# Contrail Screens Pass 2 Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Add the Live Map and Stats screens and the Bold Editorial theme to the pass 1 screen system, holding ≥ 25 fps, ≥ 55 KB free heap / ≥ 18 KB largest block, and ≥ 100 KB free flash on the CYD2USB.

**Architecture:** A per-`ScreenApp` `ScreenArena` (4 KB) holds the scratch state of whichever screen is up: the Map's land mask, airport marks and plane states, the Stats screen's text, and (moved from static memory to pay for pass 2) the Radar's blips, the Board's rows and the Sky View's stars and tracks. The base map lives in flash as tables generated by `tools/gen_map.py` (Natural Earth 1:50m land and lakes in 2°×2° tiles, OurAirports airports and runways); `basemap::LandMask` rasterises the tiles around home into a 120×135 1-bit mask with an XOR scanline fill, and the Map's frames read it. `stats::StatsBook` counts the scene at 5 Hz on the render thread and saves through `IKeyValueStore` on the network thread; the scene carries a `StatsSummary` value copy. `Theme` grows font roles, a `flat` / `brandFill` style and Map/Stats colours; `ScreenApp` owns a copy of it, so the boot setting (and the soak and bench) can pick Cinematic Sky or Bold Editorial.

**Tech Stack:** C++17, PlatformIO (`cyd2usb`, `cyd2usb_soak`, `cyd_bench`, `sim`, `test` envs), Unity tests with golden PPM images, Python 3 standard library (map generator), Pillow in `tools/.venv` (font generator, as in pass 1).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-01-contrail-screens-pass2-design.md` (pass 1: `2026-10-31-contrail-screens-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-31-contrail-screens.md`).

---

## Decisions made while planning (choices the spec leaves open, and deviations)

1. **RAM budget drives three moves into the arena.** The soak measured ≥ 59,740 B free; the target is ≥ 55 KB, so pass 2 may add only ~4.7 KB of static RAM. The arena (4,096 B) replaces the Radar's `blips_`/`prev_` (3.2 KB), the Board's `rows_` (1.5 KB) and the Sky View's `stars_`/`tracks_` (1.3 KB) as well as holding the Map's mask, marks and planes (4.0 KB) and the Stats text (0.8 KB). Net static change for all of pass 2 is about +3.0 KB (≈ 56.7 KB free expected), the logbook's type set (2.4 KB, decision 9) included. Each move is safe because every screen's `enter()` already rebuilds that state.
2. **The arena belongs to each `ScreenApp`** (a member), not a global: tests and the overlay rig run two apps side by side.
3. **Map trails are not in the arena** (spec §2 says they are). `MapTrails` (1.3 KB static) samples every scene rebuild whatever screen is up; in the arena the trails would be lost on every switch and the Map (shown 45 s at a time) would never show more than four points.
4. **Map units are 2,048 per 2° tile side** (~108 m of latitude; spec says ~25 m) **and steps are int8 deltas with an int12-pair "medium" escape (4 bytes) and an int16-pair long escape (5 bytes)** (spec: int16 deltas). Revised while building Task 6: 1:50m vertices are mostly more than 126 units apart at 4,096 units a tile (56k of 91k steps were long), so the original int8/int16 format came to ~400 KB of rings and Douglas–Peucker hardly helped (394 KB at tolerance 8). At 2,048 units with the medium step the rings are ~262 KB. The finest mask pixel is 154 m (5 nm radius), so precision is still finer than anything drawn.
   - **Antarctica is left out:** tiles entirely south of 60° S (rows 0–14, `kFirstDataRow` = 15) have no data and read as water (saves ~30 KB; nobody tracks planes there).
   - **Airports are packed 10-byte records** (24-bit latitude and longitude fractions, ~1–2 m; the ICAO code in base 37 in 21 bits; a size class; a 5-bit runway count), read through `gen::Airport::latE5()`, `lonE5()`, `ident(out)`, `large()`, `runways()` and `firstRunway()` (the latter from `kRunwayStart`, the runway count before every 32nd airport). Runways stay 8-byte records, offset from the airport's packed position. All large and medium airports are kept: ~51 KB instead of ~81 KB.
   - **Budget:** map + airport tables ≤ 360 KB (368,640 B); as generated, 342,326 B at tolerance 1.
5. **Tile cuts are recorded** (a `0x81` prefix byte on the step into the point, and bit 15 of the ring header for the closing edge) and decoded, but the mask-based coastline does not need them: a tile cut never changes the mask, so it is never drawn. The flags are there for sub-project 5's vector strokes.
6. **Tile index:** a full-land bitmap, a has-rings bitmap, a per-row rank and one offset per ring tile (≈ 15 KB), not a cell → offset table (64 KB). The cropped Antarctic rows are simply all-zero in both bitmaps (rank 0).
7. **Map scale:** the radius setting spans half the map's width (120 px); the map is 240×270 centred on home at (120, 135).
8. **Seen-today filter is a 4 KB Bloom filter with three probes.** A single bit per plane (spec §2) gives ~6 % false positives at 2,000 planes a day; three probes give ~0.5 %. It lives in RTC slow memory (`RTC_NOINIT_ATTR`) on the ESP32: no heap, and a crash reset on the same day does not recount planes. If the RTC section overflows at link time, Task 14 step 6 moves it to DRAM at 2 KB.
9. **Any valid ICAO type code counts** (the user's decision, resolving spec §3.2/§6): 2–4 characters, A–Z and 0–9, starting with a letter, and not a placeholder or ground-vehicle code (`ZZZZ`, `GRND`, `SERV`); blanks never count. Codes missing from `data::kAircraftTypes` (e.g. `C30J`) count too and show as the raw code. The set of types ever seen is capped at 400 codes and kept as two parallel sorted arrays, a packed 4-byte code (`uint32_t`, the four characters big-endian, so numeric order is alphabetical order) and a 2-byte first-sighting day. That is 6 B a code and 2,400 B in all, searched by binary search. Once 400 codes are stored, newer types still count in the top tables but are no longer first sightings. **RAM:** +1.6 KB over a table-index design. It cannot live in the arena, because counting runs whatever screen is up, and nothing else is trimmed for it. The budget still holds at ≈ 56.7 KB expected (memory plan below). If Task 14's hardware check shows `heap=` under 56 KB, the pre-planned fallback is `MapTrails::kPoints` 8 → 6 (−256 B). The callsign and time are kept only for the newest four first sightings, which is all the screen shows.
10. **Top tables** hold 16 codes each (today/all-time × airlines/types). A code not in a full table evicts the smallest entry and starts at 1.
11. **Persistence** is eight string keys (`st_day`, `st_air`, `st_typ`, `st_new`, `st_first0`…`st_first3`), each `payload#fnv1a-hex`, about 1 KB plus up to 4 KB of first sightings at the 400-code cap (`CODE:day;` ≤ 10 characters, 100 codes per `st_first` key, so each key stays ≤ ~1 KB, far under NVS's 4,000-byte string limit and within one 4 KB NVS page), written only when its text changed. Any unreadable key (bad checksum or syntax, or data without `st_day`) means start empty and log once.
12. **Counting reads the scene** (the nearest 32 airborne planes). With more than 32 in range, a plane is counted once it becomes one of the nearest 32.
13. **Threads:** counting and the summary copy run in `CoreApp::renderTick` (render thread), saving in `CoreApp::networkTick` (network thread), guarded by the book's mutex. NVS writes never block a frame.
14. **First-sighting takeover:** after the no-planes rule and the Closest Flight rule, before the high-plane Sky View rule and the rotation. Stats stays up for 20 s from when it actually appears. **At most one takeover every 5 minutes**: a new logbook sees a new type with almost every plane.
15. **Swipe and carousel order:** Closest, Radar, Map, Board, Sky, Stats.
16. **Contrast rule:** white text unless white's WCAG contrast against the fill is under 3:1 (the large-text minimum), then black. A strict "higher contrast wins" would put black text on United's blue and Delta's red, which reads worse in the mockup.
17. **The Map's strip is its own `MapStrip`** (airline · type, trend-coloured altitude) instead of a parametrised `RadarStrip`, so the Radar's goldens cannot move.
18. **Vertical swipes are a new gesture** (`Gesture::SwipeUp/SwipeDown` → `Screen::onVerticalSwipe`). On Stats either direction toggles Today / All-time.
19. **Font:** Barlow Condensed ExtraBold (SIL OFL) at 40, 28 and 18 px (`kFontCondHero/Title/Label`) through the existing `tools/gen_font.py` (Pillow in `tools/.venv`). Only the three display roles are theme fonts (`Theme::fontDisplay/fontTitle/fontLabel`). Body and small text are Inter in both themes, so no body/small roles are added (spec §2 lists them; they would be the same font).
20. **`Theme` is a value owned by `ScreenApp`** (`useTheme()` copies it and re-enters the current screen). This is not the user-facing live switch (sub-project 4). It lets the boot setting, the bench and the soak choose a theme. `editorial()` is a constant-initialised aggregate in flash, like `cinematicSky()`.
21. **Editorial headings are capitals** (`Theme::upperHeads`: "6 FLIGHTS", "QUIET SKIES").
22. **Stats All-time header:** all-time flights and "Since <first day>" (adds `flightsAll` and `startYmd` to the book).
23. **Soak in both themes = one 30-minute run** that flips the theme after every full carousel cycle (6 × 20 s) under `CONTRAIL_SOAK_THEMES`.
24. **Hardware theme pick:** a `cyd2usb_editorial` env (`-DCONTRAIL_FORCE_THEME="editorial"`), because there is no on-device settings UI yet.
25. **Namespace `basemap`** for `lib/core/map/` (no clash with `std::map` in test files).

## Conventions used by every task

- All paths are relative to `firmware/` unless they start with `tools/` or `docs/`. Run firmware commands from `firmware/`.
- One suite: `cd firmware && pio test -e test -f <suite>`. Everything: `pio test -e test`.
- **One pio env per invocation** (parallel envs race in SCons): never run two `pio run`/`pio test` commands at once.
- Goldens: `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 pio test -e test -f <suite>` writes `test/golden/<name>.ppm`. Convert for review with `../tools/.venv/bin/python ../tools/ppm2png.py test/golden/<name>.ppm "$SCRATCH/<name>.png"` (it needs Pillow, which is in `tools/.venv`) and **show the PNGs to the user before committing a new screen's goldens**. A run without `UPDATE_GOLDEN` must then pass.
- `SCRATCH` = your session scratchpad directory. Put downloads in their own new, empty directory under it, pass their paths to scripts as arguments, never run Python from inside a download directory, and run Python that reads downloads with `-I`.
- Hardware steps need the CYD2USB on `/dev/cu.usbserial-114410` and come last in their task. Flash: `pio run -e <env> -t upload --upload-port /dev/cu.usbserial-114410`. Log: run `pio device monitor -p /dev/cu.usbserial-114410 -b 115200 > "$SCRATCH/<name>.log" 2>&1` in the background and stop it when the step says. If the board is not attached, report the step as not done; do not skip ahead to the next task's hardware step.
- Namespaces: `scr` (screens), `ui` (widgets), `data` (tables), `basemap` (map data and mask), `stats` (logbook), `app` (core glue).
- Commit messages end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Commit only the files the task lists.
- Code style: match the surrounding files (4-space indent, `// ` comments that say why, 120-column lines, `constexpr` tables).

## Memory plan (where every new byte lives)

| Item | Where | Bytes |
|---|---|---|
| `ScreenArena` (per `ScreenApp`, so static in `main_esp32.cpp`) | DRAM .bss | 4,096 |
| Radar blips + previous frame, Board rows, Sky stars + tracks | moved into the arena | −6,000 |
| Map mask 120×135 bits + 20 airport marks + 32 plane states | arena | ~4,000 |
| Stats screen text | arena | ~800 |
| `MapTrails` (32 planes × 8 points) | DRAM .bss (in `ScreenApp`) | ~1,300 |
| `Theme` copy in `ScreenApp` | DRAM .bss | ~150 |
| `Scene::stats` (`StatsSummary`) | DRAM .bss (in `ScreenApp`) | ~300 |
| `StatsBook` top tables (768), type set (400 × 4-byte code + 2-byte day = 2,400), newest sightings, hourly counts | DRAM .bss (in `CoreApp`) | ~3,500 |
| Seen-today Bloom filter | RTC slow memory (`RTC_NOINIT_ATTR`) | 4,104 |
| Map tables, airports, runways | flash | ≤ 360 KB (342,326 B generated: rings 262,442, index 15,004, airports 50,810 + 318 runway starts, runways 13,752) |
| Barlow Condensed at 3 sizes | flash | ≤ 40 KB |
| Serialisation buffer for a stats key | network task stack (12 KB task) | 1,200 |

Expected free heap after pass 2: ~56.7 KB (from 59.7 KB; +4.1 KB arena, −6.0 KB moved into it, +1.3 KB trails, +0.15 KB theme, +0.3 KB scene stats, +3.5 KB logbook, ~+0.4 KB Map/Stats screen objects; the 4.1 KB seen filter is in RTC memory). Tasks 4, 11, 14 and 26 measure it on hardware.

- Test: `test/test_settings/test_main.cpp`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test.** Add this to `test/test_settings/test_main.cpp` before `main()`, and add `RUN_TEST(test_theme_setting);` in `main()`:

```cpp
static void test_theme_setting() {
    MemStore m;
    Settings s(m, kCoreSettings, kCoreSettingsCount);
    s.load();
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("cinematic", s.getEnum(key::kTheme).c_str());
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(s.set(key::kTheme, "editorial"));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("editorial", s.getEnum(key::kTheme).c_str());
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(s.set(key::kTheme, "neon"));
}
```

**Files:**

- Test: `test/test_theme/test_main.cpp` (new)

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test.** Create `test/test_theme/test_main.cpp`:

```cpp
#include <unity.h>

#include <memory>

#include "gfx/fonts/Fonts.h"
#include "screens/ScreenApp.h"
#include "screens/Theme.h"

void setUp() {}
void tearDown() {}

static void test_cinematic_roles_are_inter() {
    const scr::Theme& t = scr::cinematicSky();
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(&gfx::kFontHero, t.fontDisplay);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(&gfx::kFontTitle, t.fontTitle);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(&gfx::kFontBold, t.fontLabel);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(t.flat);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(t.brandFill);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(t.upperHeads);
}

static void test_theme_by_name() {
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(&scr::editorial(), &scr::themeByName("editorial"));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(&scr::cinematicSky(), &scr::themeByName("cinematic"));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(&scr::cinematicSky(), &scr::themeByName("bogus"));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(&scr::cinematicSky(), &scr::themeByName(nullptr));
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(scr::editorial().flat);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(scr::editorial().brandFill);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(scr::editorial().upperHeads);
}

// The app draws from its own copy, so a theme can be picked at boot (or swapped by the soak).
static void test_app_uses_a_copy_of_the_theme() {
    auto app = std::make_unique<scr::ScreenApp>();
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(app->theme().flat);
    app->useTheme(scr::editorial());
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(app->theme().flat);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(&scr::editorial() != &app->theme());
    auto ed = std::make_unique<scr::ScreenApp>(scr::editorial());
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(ed->theme().brandFill);
}

int main() {
    UNITY_BEGIN();
    RUN_TEST(test_cinematic_roles_are_inter);
    RUN_TEST(test_theme_by_name);
    RUN_TEST(test_app_uses_a_copy_of_the_theme);
    return UNITY_END();
}
```

const Theme& editorial() {
    // Placeholder until Task 18 writes the real palette: Cinematic Sky drawn flat.
    static const Theme t = [] {
        Theme e = cinematicSky();
        e.flat = e.brandFill = e.upperHeads = true;

const Theme& themeByName(const char* name) {
    return name && !std::strcmp(name, "editorial") ? editorial() : cinematicSky();
}

- [ ] **Step 3: Declare the helpers.** Append to `Widgets.h` before the closing `}  // namespace ui`:

```cpp
// Fills that honour Theme::flat. A flat theme gets solid colour (vFill: the first stop) or
// hard-edged bands (radialFill: each stop from its position out to the next), never dithering.
void vFill(gfx::Canvas& c, const gfx::Rect& r, const gfx::Rgb* stops, const float* pos, int n, const scr::Theme& t);
void radialFill(gfx::Canvas& c, const gfx::Rect& r, float cx, float cy, float rx, float ry, const gfx::Rgb* stops,
                const float* pos, int n, const scr::Theme& t);
// Additive glow; nothing at all in a flat theme.
void glow(gfx::Canvas& c, float cx, float cy, float radius, gfx::Rgb col, uint8_t peak, const scr::Theme& t);
// card() in a rounded theme; a plain square fill without the highlight in a flat one.
void panel(gfx::Canvas& c, const gfx::Rect& r, float radius, gfx::Rgb fill, gfx::Rgb edge, const scr::Theme& t);
// WCAG 2 relative luminance (0..1) and contrast ratio (1..21).
float relativeLuminance(gfx::Rgb c);
float contrastRatio(gfx::Rgb a, gfx::Rgb b);
// Text colour for a fill: white unless white falls under 3:1 against it (the WCAG minimum for
// large text), then black.
gfx::Rgb textOn(gfx::Rgb fill);
```

Also add `#include <cstdint>` under `#include <cstddef>`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement them.** Append to `Widgets.cpp` before the closing `}  // namespace ui`:

```cpp
void vFill(Canvas& c, const Rect& r, const Rgb* stops, const float* pos, int n, const scr::Theme& t) {
    if (t.flat) c.fill(r, rgb565(stops[0]));
    else c.vGradient(r, stops, pos, n);
}

void radialFill(Canvas& c, const Rect& r, float cx, float cy, float rx, float ry, const Rgb* stops, const float* pos,
                int n, const scr::Theme& t) {
    if (!t.flat) return c.radialGradient(r, cx, cy, rx, ry, stops, pos, n);
    bool same = true;
    for (int i = 1; i < n; ++i) same = same && stops[i].r == stops[0].r && stops[i].g == stops[0].g && stops[i].b == stops[0].b;
    if (same) return c.fill(r, rgb565(stops[0]));  // Editorial's black radar scope: no per-pixel work
    Rect a = r.intersect(c.clip());
    if (a.empty()) return;
    constexpr int kMax = 8;
    n = std::min(n, kMax);
    uint16_t col[kMax];
    float edge2[kMax];  // squared normalised radius where each band starts
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) col[i] = rgb565(stops[i]), edge2[i] = pos[i] * pos[i];
    const float irx = 1.f / rx, iry = 1.f / ry;
    for (int y = a.y; y < a.bottom(); ++y) {
        float dy = (y + 0.5f - cy) * iry, dy2 = dy * dy;
        uint16_t* p = &c.at(a.x, y);
        for (int x = a.x; x < a.right(); ++x) {
            float dx = (x + 0.5f - cx) * irx, d2 = dx * dx + dy2;
            int b = 0;
            while (b + 1 < n && d2 >= edge2[b + 1]) ++b;
            *p++ = col[b];
        }
    }
}

void glow(Canvas& c, float cx, float cy, float radius, Rgb col, uint8_t peak, const scr::Theme& t) {
    if (!t.flat) c.radialGlow(cx, cy, radius, col, peak);
}

void panel(Canvas& c, const Rect& r, float radius, Rgb fill, Rgb edge, const scr::Theme& t) {
    if (t.flat) c.fill(r, rgb565(fill));
    else card(c, r, radius, fill, edge);
}

namespace {
float linear(uint8_t v) {
    float s = v / 255.f;
    return s <= 0.04045f ? s / 12.92f : std::pow((s + 0.055f) / 1.055f, 2.4f);
}
}  // namespace

float relativeLuminance(Rgb c) { return 0.2126f * linear(c.r) + 0.7152f * linear(c.g) + 0.0722f * linear(c.b); }

float contrastRatio(Rgb a, Rgb b) {
    float la = relativeLuminance(a), lb = relativeLuminance(b);
    if (la < lb) std::swap(la, lb);
    return (la + 0.05f) / (lb + 0.05f);
}

Rgb textOn(Rgb fill) {
    const Rgb white{255, 255, 255}, black{0, 0, 0};
    return contrastRatio(white, fill) >= 3.f ? white : black;
}
```

`Widgets.cpp` already includes `<algorithm>` and `<cmath>`; add `#include <utility>` for `std::swap`. `Canvas::fill` clips to the canvas, so `panel` needs no intersection of its own.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the arena.** Create `lib/core/screens/ScreenArena.h`:

```cpp
#pragma once
// One scratch buffer the screens share (spec §2). Only the active screen updates and draws (the
// host dips through the veil to switch), so the screen that entered last owns it; the others
// must not touch it until they enter again, and every screen rebuilds what it keeps here in
// enter(). The largest user (the Live Map) sets the size; each user static_asserts its fit.
#include <cstddef>
#include <new>
#include <type_traits>

namespace scr {

class ScreenArena {
   public:
    static constexpr size_t kBytes = 4096;
    // A freshly constructed T for `owner`, ending any earlier owner's claim.
    template <class T>
    T* claim(const void* owner) {
        static_assert(sizeof(T) <= kBytes, "ScreenArena::kBytes is too small for this screen's scratch");
        static_assert(alignof(T) <= alignof(std::max_align_t), "over-aligned scratch");
        static_assert(std::is_trivially_destructible<T>::value, "the arena never runs destructors");
        owner_ = owner;
        return new (buf_) T();
    }
    bool owns(const void* owner) const { return owner_ == owner; }

   private:
    alignas(std::max_align_t) unsigned char buf_[kBytes];
    const void* owner_ = nullptr;
};

// A fixed-size array living in the arena. It indexes and range-fors like the member array it
// replaces, and is empty (no elements) until its screen attaches it.
template <class T, int N>
class ArenaArray {
   public:
    void attach(T* p) { p_ = p; }
    T* data() const { return p_; }
    T& operator[](int i) const { return p_[i]; }
    T* begin() const { return p_; }
    T* end() const { return p_ ? p_ + N : p_; }
    explicit operator bool() const { return p_ != nullptr; }

   private:
    T* p_ = nullptr;
};

}  // namespace scr
```

```cpp
#include <unity.h>

#include "map/Tiles.h"

using namespace basemap;

void setUp() {}
void tearDown() {}

// Four points: (100,100) → (220,100) short step → (220,2000) long step → (100,2000) short step
// marked as a tile cut; the closing edge back to (100,100) is a cut too (bit 15 of the header).
static const uint8_t kRing[] = {
    4, 0x80,                    // count 4 | closing cut
    100, 0, 100, 0,             // x0, y0
    120, 0,                     // +120, +0
    0x80, 0x00, 0x00, 0x6C, 0x07,  // long: +0, +1900
    0x81, 0x88, 0x00,           // cut, then -120, +0
};

// Short, medium and long steps, each with and without a tile cut. tools/gen_map.py --selftest encodes
// the same points and checks it gets exactly these bytes (STEP_RING), so encoder and reader agree.
static const uint8_t kSteps[] = {
    7, 0x80,                         // count 7 | closing cut
    0, 0, 0, 0,                      // (0, 0)
    0x80, 0x00, 0x08, 0x00, 0x00,    // long: +2048, +0
    0x81, 0x80, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x08,  // cut, long: +0, +2048
    0x81, 0xD0, 0xF8,                // cut, short: -48, -8
    0xF6, 0xF6,                      // short: -10, -10
    0x81, 0x82, 0x22, 0xAC, 0xBF,    // cut, medium: -990, -1030
    0x82, 0x0C, 0x0E, 0xE7,          // medium: -500, -400
};

static void test_ring_reader_decodes_steps_and_cuts() {
    RingReader rr(kRing, kRing + sizeof kRing);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(4, rr.count());
    const RingPoint want[] = {{100, 100, false}, {220, 100, false}, {220, 2000, false}, {100, 2000, true},
                              {100, 100, true}};
    RingPoint p;
    for (const RingPoint& w : want) {
        TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(rr.next(p));
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(w.x, p.x);
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(w.y, p.y);
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(w.cut, p.cut);
    }
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(rr.next(p));
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(rr.ok());
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(kRing + sizeof kRing, rr.end());
}

static void test_short_medium_and_long_steps_with_cuts() {
    RingReader rr(kSteps, kSteps + sizeof kSteps);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(7, rr.count());
    const RingPoint want[] = {{0, 0, false},       {2048, 0, false},    {2048, 2048, true}, {2000, 2040, true},
                              {1990, 2030, false}, {1000, 1000, true}, {500, 600, false},  {0, 0, true}};
    RingPoint p;
    for (const RingPoint& w : want) {
        TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(rr.next(p));
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(w.x, p.x);
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(w.y, p.y);
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(w.cut, p.cut);
    }
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(rr.next(p));
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(rr.ok());
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(kSteps + sizeof kSteps, rr.end());
    // Cut short inside the last (medium) step.
    RingReader cut(kSteps, kSteps + sizeof kSteps - 1);
    int n = 0;
    while (cut.next(p)) ++n;
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(6, n);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(cut.ok());
}

static void test_truncated_ring_is_not_ok() {
    RingReader rr(kRing, kRing + 9);  // the long step is cut short
    RingPoint p;
    int n = 0;
    while (rr.next(p)) ++n;
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(2, n);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(rr.ok());
    RingReader tiny(kRing, kRing + 3);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(tiny.next(p));
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(tiny.ok());
}

static void test_tile_cells() {
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(63, tileRow(37.6));   // 36..38
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(28, tileCol(-122.4));  // -124..-122
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(0, tileRow(-90));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(89, tileRow(90));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(0, tileCol(180));      // wraps
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(179, tileCol(-180.5));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(int(TileRef::Water), int(builtinTiles().tile(-1, 0).kind));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(int(TileRef::Water), int(builtinTiles().tile(tileRow(-75.0), tileCol(0.0)).kind));  // cropped
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(kFirstDataRow, tileRow(-60.0));
}

int main() {
    UNITY_BEGIN();
    RUN_TEST(test_ring_reader_decodes_steps_and_cuts);
    RUN_TEST(test_short_medium_and_long_steps_with_cuts);
    RUN_TEST(test_truncated_ring_is_not_ok);
    RUN_TEST(test_tile_cells);
    return UNITY_END();
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the format header.** Create `lib/core/map/Tiles.h`:

```cpp
#pragma once
// The Live Map's built-in base map (spec §2): Natural Earth 1:50m land and lakes cut into 2°×2°
// tiles, generated into flash by tools/gen_map.py (MapData.cpp). This is the format and its reader;
// LandMask rasterises it.
//
// Tile (row, col) covers latitudes [-90 + 2 row, -88 + 2 row) and longitudes [-180 + 2 col,
// -178 + 2 col). Points are tile-local, 0..kUnits on each axis (~108 m of latitude a unit), x east
// and y north. A tile is water (no data), all land (its bit in the full-land table) or a list of
// closed rings filled even-odd: land and lake rings alike, so lakes cut holes and islands in lakes
// fill again. Tiles entirely south of 60° S (Antarctica, rows below kFirstDataRow) are left out of
// the tables and so read as water.
//
// Tile data: uint16 ring count, then the rings. A ring, little-endian:
//   uint16 count | closingCut << 15   points; bit 15: the edge from the last point back to the first
//                                       is a tile cut
//   uint16 x0, uint16 y0              the first point
//   count - 1 steps, each one of
//     int8 dx, int8 dy                dx in -125..127 (0x80..0x82 never start a short step)
//     0x82, 3 bytes                   a medium step: dx and dy as int12, packed into a little-endian
//                                       24-bit value, dx in the low 12 bits
//     0x80, int16 dx, int16 dy        a long step
//     0x81, then one of the above     the edge into this point is a tile cut
// A tile cut is an edge the clipper made along the tile border: filled, never stroked. The mask
// never shows them (land lies on both sides); they are kept for sub-project 5's vector strokes.
#include <cstdint>

namespace basemap {

constexpr int kTileDeg = 2, kRows = 90, kCols = 180, kUnits = 2048;
constexpr int kFirstDataRow = 15;  // rows 0..14 (south of 60° S) hold no data

struct TileRef {
    enum Kind : uint8_t { Water, Land, Rings };
    Kind kind = Water;
    const uint8_t* data = nullptr;  // Rings: uint16 ring count, then the rings
    uint32_t size = 0;
};

struct RingPoint {
    int32_t x, y;
    bool cut;  // the edge from the previous point to this one is a tile cut
};

// Walks one ring. After the last point it reports the first again (the closing edge, with its cut
// flag), then returns false; end() is then where the next ring starts.
class RingReader {
   public:
    RingReader(const uint8_t* p, const uint8_t* end);
    bool next(RingPoint& out);
    const uint8_t* end() const { return p_; }
    int count() const { return count_; }
    bool ok() const { return ok_; }  // false if the data ran out mid-ring

   private:
    const uint8_t* p_;
    const uint8_t* end_;
    int count_ = 0, left_ = 0;
    int32_t x0_ = 0, y0_ = 0, x_ = 0, y_ = 0;
    bool closingCut_ = false, started_ = false, closed_ = false, ok_ = true;
};

// Where tiles come from: the flash tables, or a test's own.
class TileSource {
   public:
    virtual ~TileSource() = default;
    virtual TileRef tile(int row, int col) const = 0;  // Water for rows or columns out of range
};
const TileSource& builtinTiles();

int tileRow(double lat);  // clamped to 0..kRows-1
int tileCol(double lon);  // wrapped into 0..kCols-1

}  // namespace basemap
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Declare the generated tables.** Create `lib/core/map/MapData.h`:

```cpp
#pragma once
// Tables written by tools/gen_map.py into MapData.cpp. Read them through Tiles.h and Airports.h.
#include <cstdint>

#include "Tiles.h"

namespace basemap {
namespace gen {

constexpr int kBitBytes = (kRows * kCols + 7) / 8;  // one bit per tile, row-major, least significant bit first
extern const uint8_t kFullLand[kBitBytes];
extern const uint8_t kHasRings[kBitBytes];
extern const uint16_t kRowRank[kRows + 1];  // ring tiles before each row; [kRows] = all of them
extern const uint32_t kTileOffset[];        // per ring tile, into kRingData, plus the end
extern const uint8_t kRingData[];
extern const uint32_t kRingDataSize;

// OurAirports large and medium airports, sorted by latitude, as packed 10-byte records (about 51 KB
// for ~5,100 airports instead of 81 KB as plain structs). Little-endian bits:
//   0-23   latitude, (lat + 90) / 180 of 2^24      (~1.1 m)
//   24-47  longitude, (lon + 180) / 360 of 2^24    (~2.4 m at the equator)
//   48-68  ICAO code, base 37 from the first character: 0 pads a 3-character code, 1-10 are '0'-'9',
//          11-36 are 'A'-'Z'
//   69-70  size class: 1 large, 0 medium
//   71-75  runway count (large airports only, at most 31)
struct Airport {
    uint8_t b[10];

    int32_t latE5() const;  // degrees × 1e5
    int32_t lonE5() const;
    void ident(char out[5]) const;  // NUL-terminated
    bool large() const { return (b[8] >> 5 & 3) == 1; }
    uint8_t runways() const { return uint8_t((b[8] >> 7 | b[9] << 1) & 31); }
    uint16_t firstRunway() const;  // into kRunways
};
struct Runway {
    int16_t dLat1, dLon1, dLat2, dLon2;  // the two ends, 1e-5 degrees from the airport's packed position
};
extern const Airport kAirports[];
extern const int kAirportCount;
constexpr int kRunwayBlock = 32;
extern const uint16_t kRunwayStart[];  // runways before airport kRunwayBlock × i
extern const Runway kRunways[];
extern const int kRunwayCount;

inline int32_t Airport::latE5() const {
    const uint32_t v = uint32_t(b[0]) | uint32_t(b[1]) << 8 | uint32_t(b[2]) << 16;
    return int32_t((int64_t(v) * 18000000 + (1 << 23)) >> 24) - 9000000;
}

inline int32_t Airport::lonE5() const {
    const uint32_t v = uint32_t(b[3]) | uint32_t(b[4]) << 8 | uint32_t(b[5]) << 16;
    return int32_t((int64_t(v) * 36000000 + (1 << 23)) >> 24) - 18000000;
}

inline void Airport::ident(char out[5]) const {
    static constexpr char kChars[] = "0123456789ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ";
    uint32_t v = (uint32_t(b[6]) | uint32_t(b[7]) << 8 | uint32_t(b[8]) << 16) & 0x1FFFFF;
    int n = 0;
    for (; v && n < 4; v /= 37)
        if (v % 37) out[n++] = kChars[v % 37 - 1];
    out[n] = 0;
}

inline uint16_t Airport::firstRunway() const {
    const int i = int(this - kAirports), block = i / kRunwayBlock;
    int n = kRunwayStart[block];
    for (int j = block * kRunwayBlock; j < i; ++j) n += kAirports[j].runways();
    return uint16_t(n);
}

}  // namespace gen
}  // namespace basemap
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the reader.** Create `lib/core/map/Tiles.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "Tiles.h"

#include <cmath>

#include "MapData.h"

namespace basemap {

namespace {

uint16_t rd16(const uint8_t* p) { return uint16_t(p[0] | p[1] << 8); }
bool bit(const uint8_t* bits, int i) { return bits[i >> 3] >> (i & 7) & 1; }

class FlashTiles : public TileSource {
   public:
    TileRef tile(int row, int col) const override {
        TileRef t;
        if (row < 0 || row >= kRows || col < 0 || col >= kCols) return t;
        const int i = row * kCols + col;
        if (bit(gen::kFullLand, i)) return t.kind = TileRef::Land, t;
        if (!bit(gen::kHasRings, i)) return t;
        // The tile's rank among ring tiles: the row's start plus the ring tiles before it in the row.
        int k = gen::kRowRank[row];
        for (int j = row * kCols; j < i; ++j) k += bit(gen::kHasRings, j);
        t.kind = TileRef::Rings;
        t.data = gen::kRingData + gen::kTileOffset[k];
        t.size = gen::kTileOffset[k + 1] - gen::kTileOffset[k];
        return t;
    }
};

}  // namespace

RingReader::RingReader(const uint8_t* p, const uint8_t* end) : p_(p), end_(end) {
    if (end_ - p_ < 6) {
        ok_ = false;
        return;
    }
    uint16_t head = rd16(p_);
    count_ = left_ = head & 0x7FFF, closingCut_ = head >> 15;
    x_ = x0_ = rd16(p_ + 2), y_ = y0_ = rd16(p_ + 4);
    p_ += 6;
}

bool RingReader::next(RingPoint& o) {
    if (!ok_ || count_ == 0) return false;
    if (!started_) {
        started_ = true, --left_;
        o = {x0_, y0_, false};
        return true;
    }
    if (left_ > 0) {
        bool cut = p_ < end_ && *p_ == 0x81;
        if (cut) ++p_;
        if (p_ < end_ && *p_ == 0x80) {
            if (end_ - p_ < 5) return ok_ = false;
            x_ += int16_t(rd16(p_ + 1)), y_ += int16_t(rd16(p_ + 3)), p_ += 5;
        } else if (p_ < end_ && *p_ == 0x82) {
            if (end_ - p_ < 4) return ok_ = false;
            const uint32_t v = uint32_t(p_[1]) | uint32_t(p_[2]) << 8 | uint32_t(p_[3]) << 16;
            x_ += int32_t((v & 0xFFF) ^ 0x800) - 0x800, y_ += int32_t((v >> 12) ^ 0x800) - 0x800, p_ += 4;
        } else {
            if (end_ - p_ < 2) return ok_ = false;
            x_ += int8_t(p_[0]), y_ += int8_t(p_[1]), p_ += 2;
        }
        --left_;
        o = {x_, y_, cut};
        return true;
    }
    if (closed_) return false;
    closed_ = true;
    o = {x0_, y0_, closingCut_};
    return true;
}

const TileSource& builtinTiles() {
    static const FlashTiles tiles;
    return tiles;
}

int tileRow(double lat) {
    int r = int(std::floor((lat + 90) / kTileDeg));
    return r < 0 ? 0 : r >= kRows ? kRows - 1 : r;
}

int tileCol(double lon) {
    int c = int(std::floor((lon + 180) / kTileDeg)) % kCols;
    return c < 0 ? c + kCols : c;
}

}  // namespace basemap
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the generator.** Create `tools/gen_map.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build firmware/lib/core/map/MapData.cpp: the Live Map's built-in base map.

Natural Earth 1:50m land and lakes are cut into 2x2-degree tiles (Sutherland-Hodgman, a latitude
band at a time, then a column at a time), quantised to 2048 units per tile side (~108 m) and
delta-coded in the ring format documented in firmware/lib/core/map/Tiles.h (int8 short steps, int12
medium steps, int16 long steps). Edges the clipper makes along a tile border are flagged as tile cuts
(filled, never stroked). Tiles entirely south of 60 S (Antarctica) are left out. OurAirports large
and medium airports follow as packed 10-byte records (see firmware/lib/core/map/MapData.h), with the
runways of the large ones as a flat table.

The inputs are local files (see the plan, Task 6); only the Python standard library is needed. The
output is committed, so firmware builds never need this script or the data.

  python3 -I tools/gen_map.py --land ne_50m_land.geojson --lakes ne_50m_lakes.geojson \\
      --airports airports.csv --runways runways.csv --out firmware/lib/core/map/MapData.cpp \\
      [--tolerance UNITS] [--budget BYTES] [--probe LAT,LON ...]
  python3 -I tools/gen_map.py --selftest

Exit codes: 0 written, 2 over the flash budget (nothing written), 3 a known point came out wrong.
"""
import argparse
import csv
import json
import math
import re
import struct
import sys

TILE_DEG = 2
ROWS, COLS = 90, 180
UNITS = 2048
MIN_ROW = 15  # rows 0..14 lie entirely south of 60 S (Antarctica): left out
BIT_BYTES = (ROWS * COLS + 7) // 8
DEFAULT_BUDGET = 360 * 1024
# Points the output must get right, checked on the encoded tiles: (name, lat, lon, land?).
KNOWN = [
    ("Kansas", 38.5, -98.5, True),
    ("Cambridge, MA", 42.373, -71.119, True),
    ("Lake Superior", 47.7, -87.5, False),
    ("Caspian Sea", 42.0, 50.5, False),
    ("North Pacific", 30.5, -140.5, False),
    ("Antarctica (left out)", -75.0, 0.0, False),
]


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ input

def polygons(path):
    """Each polygon in a GeoJSON file as a list of rings [(lon, lat), ...], outline first."""
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        doc = json.load(f)
    for feat in doc["features"]:
        g = feat.get("geometry") or {}
        if g.get("type") == "Polygon":
            polys = [g["coordinates"]]
        elif g.get("type") == "MultiPolygon":
            polys = g["coordinates"]
        else:
            continue
        for poly in polys:
            rings = []
            for ring in poly:
                pts = [(float(p[0]), float(p[1])) for p in ring]
                if len(pts) > 1 and pts[0] == pts[-1]:
                    pts.pop()
                if len(pts) >= 3:
                    rings.append(pts)
            if rings:
                yield rings


def bbox(ring):
    xs, ys = [p[0] for p in ring], [p[1] for p in ring]
    return min(xs), min(ys), max(xs), max(ys)


def load_geometry(land_path, lakes_path):
    """Every ring to fill even-odd: land outlines and holes, then the lakes that are not already
    holes in the land (a lake drawn both ways would cancel out and come back as land)."""
    land, holes = [], []
    for rings in polygons(land_path):
        land.extend(rings)
        holes.extend(bbox(r) for r in rings[1:])
    lakes, dropped = [], 0
    for rings in polygons(lakes_path):
        for r in rings:
            b = bbox(r)
            if any(all(abs(b[i] - h[i]) < 0.05 for i in range(4)) for h in holes):
                dropped += 1
                continue
            lakes.append(r)
    return land, lakes, dropped


CODE = re.compile(r"^[A-Z0-9]{3,4}$")
IDENT_CHARS = "0123456789ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ"  # base 37: 0 pads a short code, then these
MAX_RUNWAYS = 31  # 5 bits in the record
RUNWAY_BLOCK = 32  # kRunwayStart holds the runways before every 32nd airport


def lat24(lat):
    return min((1 << 24) - 1, max(0, round((lat + 90) / 180 * (1 << 24))))


def lon24(lon):
    return round((lon + 180) / 360 * (1 << 24)) % (1 << 24)


def lat_e5(v):
    """A packed latitude back in 1e-5 degrees, exactly as MapData.h's Airport::latE5() does it."""
    return ((v * 18000000 + (1 << 23)) >> 24) - 9000000


def lon_e5(v):
    return ((v * 36000000 + (1 << 23)) >> 24) - 18000000


def ident21(code):
    v = 0
    for i, ch in enumerate(code):
        v += (1 + IDENT_CHARS.index(ch)) * 37 ** i
    return v


def unident21(v):
    out = ""
    while v:
        v, d = divmod(v, 37)
        out += IDENT_CHARS[d - 1] if d else ""
    return out


def pack_airport(a):
    """The 10-byte record: bits 0-23 latitude, 24-47 longitude (both unsigned fractions of the
    range), 48-68 ident (base 37), 69-70 size class (1 large, 0 medium), 71-75 runway count."""
    v = (a["lat24"] | a["lon24"] << 24 | ident21(a["code"]) << 48 | (1 if a["large"] else 0) << 69
         | len(a["rw"]) << 71)
    return v.to_bytes(10, "little")


def unpack_airport(b):
    v = int.from_bytes(b, "little")
    return {"latE5": lat_e5(v & 0xFFFFFF), "lonE5": lon_e5(v >> 24 & 0xFFFFFF), "code": unident21(v >> 48 & 0x1FFFFF),
            "large": bool(v >> 69 & 3), "runways": v >> 71 & 31}


def load_airports(path, runways_path):
    """Large and medium airports sorted by packed (lat, lon); large ones carry runway end offsets,
    in 1e-5 degrees from the airport's packed position (so the drawn runways stay exact)."""
    aps = []
    with open(path, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for r in csv.DictReader(f):
            if r["type"] not in ("large_airport", "medium_airport"):
                continue
            code = next((c for c in (r.get("icao_code") or "", r.get("gps_code") or "", r["ident"]) if CODE.match(c)),
                        None)
            if not code:
                continue
            lat, lon = float(r["latitude_deg"]), float(r["longitude_deg"])
            a = {"ident": r["ident"], "code": code, "large": r["type"] == "large_airport",
                 "lat24": lat24(lat), "lon24": lon24(lon), "rw": []}
            a["latE5"], a["lonE5"] = lat_e5(a["lat24"]), lon_e5(a["lon24"])
            aps.append(a)
    large = {a["ident"]: a for a in aps if a["large"]}
    with open(runways_path, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for r in csv.DictReader(f):
            a = large.get(r["airport_ident"])
            if not a or r.get("closed") == "1":
                continue
            try:
                lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2 = (float(r[k]) for k in ("le_latitude_deg", "le_longitude_deg",
                                                                "he_latitude_deg", "he_longitude_deg"))
            except ValueError:
                continue
            d = [round(lat1 * 1e5) - a["latE5"], round(lon1 * 1e5) - a["lonE5"],
                 round(lat2 * 1e5) - a["latE5"], round(lon2 * 1e5) - a["lonE5"]]
            if all(-32768 <= v <= 32767 for v in d) and len(a["rw"]) < MAX_RUNWAYS:
                a["rw"].append(d)
    aps.sort(key=lambda a: (a["lat24"], a["lon24"]))
    return aps


def runway_starts(airports):
    starts, n = [], 0
    for i, a in enumerate(airports):
        if i % RUNWAY_BLOCK == 0:
            starts.append(n)
        n += len(a["rw"])
    return starts or [0]


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ geometry

def area(poly):
    s = 0.0
    for (x0, y0), (x1, y1) in zip(poly, poly[1:] + poly[:1]):
        s += x0 * y1 - x1 * y0
    return s / 2


def simplify(ring, tol):
    """Douglas-Peucker on a closed ring (tolerance in degrees); the ring stays closed."""
    if tol <= 0 or len(ring) < 8:
        return ring
    x0, y0 = ring[0]
    far = max(range(len(ring)), key=lambda i: (ring[i][0] - x0) ** 2 + (ring[i][1] - y0) ** 2)
    keep = [False] * len(ring)
    keep[0] = keep[far] = True
    n = len(ring)
    for a, b in ((0, far), (far, n)):
        stack = [(a, b)]
        while stack:
            i, j = stack.pop()
            ax, ay = ring[i]
            bx, by = ring[j % n]
            dx, dy = bx - ax, by - ay
            norm = math.hypot(dx, dy)
            best, at = -1.0, -1
            for k in range(i + 1, j):
                px, py = ring[k]
                d = abs(dy * (px - ax) - dx * (py - ay)) / norm if norm else math.hypot(px - ax, py - ay)
                if d > best:
                    best, at = d, k
            if at >= 0 and best > tol:
                keep[at] = True
                stack.append((i, at))
                stack.append((at, j))
    out = [p for p, k in zip(ring, keep) if k]
    return out if len(out) >= 3 else ring


def clip(poly, axis, value, keep_low):
    """Sutherland-Hodgman against one axis-aligned line. Points made on the line get exactly
    `value` on that axis, so tile-cut edges can be recognised by exact comparison."""
    out = []
    if not poly:
        return out
    inside = (lambda p: p[axis] <= value) if keep_low else (lambda p: p[axis] >= value)
    prev = poly[-1]
    pin = inside(prev)
    for cur in poly:
        cin = inside(cur)
        if cin != pin:
            t = (value - prev[axis]) / (cur[axis] - prev[axis])
            q = [0.0, 0.0]
            q[axis] = value
            q[1 - axis] = prev[1 - axis] + (cur[1 - axis] - prev[1 - axis]) * t
            out.append((q[0], q[1]))
        if cin:
            out.append(cur)
        prev, pin = cur, cin
    return out


def tile_rings(rings):
    """{(row, col): [piece in degrees, ...]} for every tile the rings reach."""
    tiles = {}
    for ring in rings:
        lats = [p[1] for p in ring]
        r0 = max(MIN_ROW, int(math.floor((min(lats) + 90) / TILE_DEG)))
        r1 = min(ROWS - 1, int(math.floor((max(lats) + 90) / TILE_DEG)))
        for row in range(r0, r1 + 1):
            lat0 = -90 + row * TILE_DEG
            band = clip(clip(ring, 1, lat0, False), 1, lat0 + TILE_DEG, True)
            if len(band) < 3:
                continue
            lons = [p[0] for p in band]
            c0 = max(0, int(math.floor((min(lons) + 180) / TILE_DEG)))
            c1 = min(COLS - 1, int(math.floor((max(lons) + 180) / TILE_DEG)))
            for col in range(c0, c1 + 1):
                lon0 = -180 + col * TILE_DEG
                piece = clip(clip(band, 0, lon0, False), 0, lon0 + TILE_DEG, True)
                if len(piece) >= 3 and abs(area(piece)) > 1e-12:
                    tiles.setdefault((row, col), []).append(piece)
    return tiles


def quantise(piece, row, col):
    """Tile-local integer points [(x, y, cut)], cut meaning the edge INTO the point is a tile cut;
    point 0 carries the closing edge (last -> first). [] if fewer than 3 distinct points remain."""
    lat0, lon0 = -90 + row * TILE_DEG, -180 + col * TILE_DEG
    lo, hi = (lon0, lat0), (lon0 + TILE_DEG, lat0 + TILE_DEG)

    def on_border(a, b):
        # Both ends on the same tile edge: the clipper's cut (or the dataset's seam at 180 degrees).
        return any(a[ax] == b[ax] and (a[ax] == lo[ax] or a[ax] == hi[ax]) for ax in (0, 1))

    pts = []
    for i, p in enumerate(piece):
        x = min(UNITS, max(0, round((p[0] - lon0) / TILE_DEG * UNITS)))
        y = min(UNITS, max(0, round((p[1] - lat0) / TILE_DEG * UNITS)))
        pts.append((x, y, on_border(piece[i - 1], p)))
    out = []
    for x, y, cut in pts:
        if out and out[-1][0] == x and out[-1][1] == y:
            continue  # a repeat: the edge into the next point keeps its own flag
        out.append((x, y, cut))
    while len(out) > 1 and out[0][:2] == out[-1][:2]:
        last = out.pop()
        out[0] = (out[0][0], out[0][1], last[2])  # the closing edge is now the one into the popped point
    return out if len(out) >= 3 else []


def is_full(q):
    """A quantised ring that is the whole tile: every edge a cut, and the tile's area."""
    s = 0
    for (x0, y0, _), (x1, y1, _) in zip(q, q[1:] + q[:1]):
        s += x0 * y1 - x1 * y0
    return all(c for _, _, c in q) and abs(s) == 2 * UNITS * UNITS


def inside(rings, x, y):
    """Even-odd point-in-rings (any (x, y, ...) point tuples)."""
    c = False
    for ring in rings:
        for i in range(len(ring)):
            x0, y0 = ring[i - 1][0], ring[i - 1][1]
            x1, y1 = ring[i][0], ring[i][1]
            if (y0 > y) != (y1 > y) and x < x0 + (y - y0) * (x1 - x0) / (y1 - y0):
                c = not c
    return c


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ encoding

def encode_ring(q):
    n = len(q)
    assert 3 <= n < 0x8000, n
    out = bytearray(struct.pack("<HHH", n | (0x8000 if q[0][2] else 0), q[0][0], q[0][1]))
    px, py = q[0][0], q[0][1]
    for x, y, cut in q[1:]:
        dx, dy = x - px, y - py
        if cut:
            out.append(0x81)
        if -125 <= dx <= 127 and -128 <= dy <= 127:  # 0x80..0x82 never start a short step
            out += struct.pack("<bb", dx, dy)
        elif -2048 <= dx <= 2047 and -2048 <= dy <= 2047:
            out.append(0x82)
            out += ((dx & 0xFFF) | (dy & 0xFFF) << 12).to_bytes(3, "little")
        else:
            out.append(0x80)
            out += struct.pack("<hh", dx, dy)
        px, py = x, y
    return out


def encode_tile(rings):
    return struct.pack("<H", len(rings)) + b"".join(encode_ring(q) for q in rings)


def decode_tile(data):
    """The inverse of encode_tile: ([[(x, y, cut), ...], ...], bytes used)."""
    (n,) = struct.unpack_from("<H", data, 0)
    p, rings = 2, []
    for _ in range(n):
        head, x, y = struct.unpack_from("<HHH", data, p)
        p += 6
        pts = [(x, y, bool(head & 0x8000))]
        for _ in range((head & 0x7FFF) - 1):
            cut = data[p] == 0x81
            if cut:
                p += 1
            if data[p] == 0x80:
                dx, dy = struct.unpack_from("<hh", data, p + 1)
                p += 5
            elif data[p] == 0x82:
                v = int.from_bytes(data[p + 1:p + 4], "little")
                dx, dy = ((v & 0xFFF) ^ 0x800) - 0x800, ((v >> 12) ^ 0x800) - 0x800
                p += 4
            else:
                dx, dy = struct.unpack_from("<bb", data, p)
                p += 2
            x, y = x + dx, y + dy
            pts.append((x, y, cut))
        rings.append(pts)
    return rings, p


def build_tiles(land, lakes, tol_units):
    """(full-land tiles, {tile: encoded rings})."""
    tol = tol_units * TILE_DEG / UNITS
    land_t = tile_rings([simplify(r, tol) for r in land])
    lake_t = tile_rings([simplify(r, tol) for r in lakes])
    full, rings = set(), {}
    for key in set(land_t) | set(lake_t):
        row, col = key
        lq = [q for q in (quantise(p, row, col) for p in land_t.get(key, [])) if q]
        kq = [q for q in (quantise(p, row, col) for p in lake_t.get(key, [])) if q]
        if not kq and len(lq) == 1 and is_full(lq[0]):
            full.add(key)
        elif lq or kq:
            rings[key] = encode_tile(lq + kq)
    return full, rings


def land_at(full, rings, lat, lon):
    """Land or water on the encoded tiles, as the firmware's mask sees it."""
    row, col = int((lat + 90) // TILE_DEG), int((lon + 180) // TILE_DEG)
    if (row, col) in full:
        return True
    if (row, col) not in rings:
        return False
    x = (lon - (-180 + col * TILE_DEG)) / TILE_DEG * UNITS
    y = (lat - (-90 + row * TILE_DEG)) / TILE_DEG * UNITS
    return inside(decode_tile(rings[(row, col)])[0], x, y)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ output

def tables(full, rings):
    full_bits, ring_bits = bytearray(BIT_BYTES), bytearray(BIT_BYTES)
    for r, c in full:
        i = r * COLS + c
        full_bits[i >> 3] |= 1 << (i & 7)
    offsets, data, rank = [], bytearray(), []
    for row in range(ROWS):
        rank.append(len(offsets))
        for col in range(COLS):
            if (row, col) in rings:
                i = row * COLS + col
                ring_bits[i >> 3] |= 1 << (i & 7)
                offsets.append(len(data))
                data += rings[(row, col)]
    rank.append(len(offsets))
    offsets.append(len(data))
    return full_bits, ring_bits, rank, offsets, data


def flash_bytes(t, airports):
    full_bits, ring_bits, rank, offsets, data = t
    runways = sum(len(a["rw"]) for a in airports)
    return (len(data) + 4 * len(offsets) + len(full_bits) + len(ring_bits) + 2 * len(rank) + 10 * len(airports)
            + 2 * len(runway_starts(airports)) + 8 * runways)


def c_rows(values, per=32):
    vals = list(values) or [0]
    return ["    " + ",".join(str(v) for v in vals[i:i + per]) + "," for i in range(0, len(vals), per)]


def write_cpp(path, t, airports, note):
    full_bits, ring_bits, rank, offsets, data = t
    out = ["// Generated by tools/gen_map.py. Do not edit.",
           "// Land and lakes: Natural Earth 1:50m (public domain, naturalearthdata.com).",
           "// Airports and runways: OurAirports (public domain, ourairports.com).",
           "// " + note,
           '#include "MapData.h"', "", "namespace basemap {", "namespace gen {", ""]

    def array(decl, values):
        out.append(decl + " = {")
        out.extend(c_rows(values))
        out.append("};")

    array("const uint8_t kFullLand[kBitBytes]", full_bits)
    array("const uint8_t kHasRings[kBitBytes]", ring_bits)
    array("const uint16_t kRowRank[kRows + 1]", rank)
    array("const uint32_t kTileOffset[]", offsets)
    array("const uint8_t kRingData[]", data)
    out.append(f"const uint32_t kRingDataSize = {len(data)};")
    out.append("const Airport kAirports[] = {")
    runways = []
    for a in airports:
        out.append("    {{" + ",".join(str(b) for b in pack_airport(a)) + "}},  // " + a["code"])
        runways.extend(a["rw"])
    if not airports:
        out.append("    {{0}},")
    out.append("};")
    out.append(f"const int kAirportCount = {len(airports)};")
    array("const uint16_t kRunwayStart[]", runway_starts(airports))
    out.append("const Runway kRunways[] = {")
    out.extend(f"    {{{d[0]}, {d[1]}, {d[2]}, {d[3]}}}," for d in runways)
    if not runways:
        out.append("    {0, 0, 0, 0},")
    out.append("};")
    out.append(f"const int kRunwayCount = {len(runways)};")
    out += ["", "}  // namespace gen", "}  // namespace basemap", ""]
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write("\n".join(out))


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ self-test

STEP_RING = [(0, 0, True), (2048, 0, False), (2048, 2048, True), (2000, 2040, True), (1990, 2030, False),
             (1000, 1000, True), (500, 600, False)]
STEP_RING_BYTES = "07 80 00 00 00 00 80 00 08 00 00 81 80 00 00 00 08 81 d0 f8 f6 f6 81 82 22 ac bf 82 0c 0e e7"

def selftest():
    def sq(x0, y0, x1, y1):
        return [(x0, y0), (x1, y0), (x1, y1), (x0, y1)]

    t = tile_rings([sq(0, 0, 4, 4)])  # four whole tiles
    assert sorted(t) == [(45, 90), (45, 91), (46, 90), (46, 91)], sorted(t)
    for (row, col), pieces in t.items():
        assert len(pieces) == 1 and is_full(quantise(pieces[0], row, col)), (row, col)
    t = tile_rings([sq(0.5, 0.5, 1.5, 1.5)])  # an island: no cuts
    q = quantise(t[(45, 90)][0], 45, 90)
    assert len(q) == 4 and not any(c for _, _, c in q), q
    t = tile_rings([sq(1, 0.5, 3, 1.5)])  # across lon = 2: one cut on each side, on that border
    for key, border in (((45, 90), UNITS), ((45, 91), 0)):
        q = quantise(t[key][0], *key)
        cuts = [(x, y) for x, y, c in q if c]
        assert len(cuts) == 1 and cuts[0][0] == border, (key, q)
    rings = [[(10, 10, True), (20, 15, False), (25, 40, True), (5, 30, False)],
             [(0, 0, False), (1500, 0, False), (1500, 2048, True), (0, 2048, False)]]
    data = encode_tile(rings)
    back, used = decode_tile(data)
    assert back == rings and used == len(data), back
    # Short, medium and long steps, each with and without a cut: the same bytes as kSteps in
    # firmware/test/test_map_tiles, so the encoder and the firmware's reader agree.
    data = encode_ring(STEP_RING)
    assert data == bytes.fromhex(STEP_RING_BYTES), data.hex(" ")
    assert decode_tile(b"\x01\x00" + data) == ([STEP_RING], len(data) + 2)
    assert not tile_rings([sq(0, -70, 4, -61)]), "Antarctica is left out"
    t = tile_rings([sq(0, -61, 2, -59)])
    assert sorted(t) == [(MIN_ROW, 90)], sorted(t)  # the band north of 60 S stays
    a = {"code": "KSFO", "large": True, "lat24": lat24(37.618999), "lon24": lon24(-122.375), "rw": [[0, 0, 0, 0]] * 4}
    u = unpack_airport(pack_airport(a))
    assert u["code"] == "KSFO" and u["large"] and u["runways"] == 4, u
    assert abs(u["latE5"] - 3761900) <= 1 and abs(u["lonE5"] + 12237500) <= 2, u
    for code in ("EGLL", "0A9", "ZZZZ", "999", "A00"):
        assert unident21(ident21(code)) == code and ident21(code) < 1 << 21, code
    for lat in (-90, -0.00001, 0, 45.5, 90):
        assert abs(lat_e5(lat24(lat)) - lat * 1e5) <= 1, lat
    for lon in (-180, -0.00001, 0, 179.999):  # 179.99999 packs as -180, the same meridian
        assert abs(lon_e5(lon24(lon)) - lon * 1e5) <= 2, lon
    full, enc = build_tiles([sq(0.5, 0.5, 1.5, 1.5)], [sq(0.9, 0.9, 1.1, 1.1)], 0)  # an island with a lake
    assert land_at(full, enc, 0.7, 0.7) and not land_at(full, enc, 1.0, 1.0) and not land_at(full, enc, 0.2, 0.2)
    full, enc = build_tiles([sq(0, 0, 4, 4)], [], 0)
    assert full == {(45, 90), (45, 91), (46, 90), (46, 91)} and not enc, (full, enc)
    print("selftest ok")


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ main

def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    for name in ("land", "lakes", "airports", "runways", "out"):
        ap.add_argument("--" + name)
    ap.add_argument("--tolerance", type=float, default=1.0, help="Douglas-Peucker tolerance in tile units (~108 m)")
    ap.add_argument("--budget", type=int, default=DEFAULT_BUDGET, help="flash bytes allowed for all tables")
    ap.add_argument("--probe", action="append", default=[], metavar="LAT,LON")
    ap.add_argument("--selftest", action="store_true")
    a = ap.parse_args()
    if a.selftest:
        selftest()
        return 0
    missing = [n for n in ("land", "lakes", "airports", "runways", "out") if not getattr(a, n)]
    if missing:
        ap.error("missing " + ", ".join("--" + n for n in missing))
    land, lakes, dropped = load_geometry(a.land, a.lakes)
    print(f"{len(land)} land rings, {len(lakes)} lake rings ({dropped} lakes are already holes in the land)")
    full, rings = build_tiles(land, lakes, a.tolerance)
    airports = load_airports(a.airports, a.runways)
    for spec in a.probe:
        lat, lon = (float(v) for v in spec.split(","))
        src = "land" if inside(land + lakes, lon, lat) else "water"
        enc = "land" if land_at(full, rings, lat, lon) else "water"
        print(f"probe {lat},{lon}: source {src}, tiles {enc}")
    bad = [n for n, lat, lon, want in KNOWN if land_at(full, rings, lat, lon) != want]
    if bad:
        print("wrong land/water at: " + ", ".join(bad))
        return 3
    t = tables(full, rings)
    size = flash_bytes(t, airports)
    runways = sum(len(x["rw"]) for x in airports)
    note = (f"tolerance {a.tolerance} units; {len(full)} full tiles, {len(rings)} ring tiles, {len(t[4])} ring bytes; "
            f"{len(airports)} airports, {runways} runways; {size} bytes in all")
    print(note)
    if size > a.budget:
        print(f"over the {a.budget}-byte budget: rerun with a larger --tolerance")
        return 2
    write_cpp(a.out, t, airports, note)
    print(f"wrote {a.out}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the self-test.** From the repo root: `python3 -I tools/gen_map.py --selftest`. Expected: `selftest ok`. If an assertion fails, fix the generator before going on: the firmware's format test (Task 5) and this self-test must agree.

- [ ] **Step 3: Download the data** (approved by the user) into a new, empty directory:

```bash
SRC="$SCRATCH/mapsrc"; mkdir "$SRC"
curl -fL -o "$SRC/ne_50m_land.geojson"  https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nvkelso/natural-earth-vector/master/geojson/ne_50m_land.geojson
curl -fL -o "$SRC/ne_50m_lakes.geojson" https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nvkelso/natural-earth-vector/master/geojson/ne_50m_lakes.geojson

ls -l "$SRC"
```

Expected: four non-empty files (roughly 1–4 MB GeoJSON each, ~12 MB and ~4 MB CSV). These files are data. Nothing in them is run, and nothing is run from that directory.

- [ ] **Step 4: Generate, with probes for the Boston test points.** From the repo root (not from `$SRC`):

```bash
python3 -I tools/gen_map.py --land "$SRC/ne_50m_land.geojson" --lakes "$SRC/ne_50m_lakes.geojson" \
    --airports "$SRC/airports.csv" --runways "$SRC/runways.csv" --out firmware/lib/core/map/MapData.cpp \
    --probe 42.335,-70.955 --probe 42.373,-71.119
```

Expected: exit 0, a summary line ending `… bytes in all` at or under 368,640 (as built: 342,326 at tolerance 1), and `probe 42.373,-71.119: … tiles land` (Cambridge). Record the harbor probe. If it says `tiles land` (the 1:50m coastline is coarse at Boston), rerun with `--probe` at longitudes −70.935, −70.915, −70.895 … (steps of 0.02°, same latitude) and use the first that says `tiles water` in Task 7's test. The generator takes a minute or two. If it exits 3 (a known point came out wrong), rerun with `--probe` at that point: if `source` already says the wrong thing, the 1:50m data is coarse there. Replace that `KNOWN` entry with a nearby point that is unambiguous at 1:50m (for Cambridge, Somerville at 42.39,-71.10) and say so in the commit message. If `source` is right but `tiles` is wrong, the clipper or encoder has a bug: fix it and add the case to `selftest()`.

- [ ] **Step 5: If it is over budget (exit 2), simplify.** Rerun the same command with `--tolerance 2`, then 4, then 8, stopping at the first that exits 0. Each step trades coastline detail (1 unit ≈ 108 m, 2 ≈ 220 m, 8 ≈ 870 m) for size. The finest mask pixel is 154 m, so tolerance 1 cannot be seen and 2 hardly. If 8 is still over budget, stop and report. Record the tolerance used. (As built: tolerance 1 fits.)

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the data test.** Create `test/test_map_data/test_main.cpp`:

```cpp
#include <unity.h>

#include <cstring>

#include "map/MapData.h"
#include "map/Tiles.h"

using namespace basemap;

void setUp() {}
void tearDown() {}

static int popcount(const uint8_t* bits) {
    int n = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < gen::kBitBytes; ++i) n += __builtin_popcount(bits[i]);
    return n;
}

static void test_index_is_consistent() {
    const int ringTiles = popcount(gen::kHasRings);
    TEST_ASSERT_GREATER_THAN(500, ringTiles);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(ringTiles, gen::kRowRank[kRows]);
    for (int r = 0; r < kRows; ++r) TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(gen::kRowRank[r] <= gen::kRowRank[r + 1]);
    for (int k = 0; k < ringTiles; ++k) TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(gen::kTileOffset[k] < gen::kTileOffset[k + 1]);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_UINT32(gen::kRingDataSize, gen::kTileOffset[ringTiles]);
    TEST_ASSERT_GREATER_THAN(1000, popcount(gen::kFullLand));
    for (int i = 0; i < gen::kBitBytes; ++i) TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_HEX8(0, gen::kFullLand[i] & gen::kHasRings[i]);
}

// Every ring tile decodes to its exact end, with every point inside the tile.
static void test_every_tile_decodes() {
    int rings = 0;
    for (int r = 0; r < kRows; ++r)
        for (int c = 0; c < kCols; ++c) {
            TileRef t = builtinTiles().tile(r, c);
            if (t.kind != TileRef::Rings) continue;
            const uint8_t* p = t.data + 2;
            const uint8_t* end = t.data + t.size;
            const int n = t.data[0] | t.data[1] << 8;
            TEST_ASSERT_GREATER_THAN(0, n);
            for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
                RingReader rr(p, end);
                RingPoint pt;
                int pts = 0;
                while (rr.next(pt)) {
                    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(pt.x >= 0 && pt.x <= kUnits && pt.y >= 0 && pt.y <= kUnits);
                    ++pts;
                }
                TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(rr.ok());
                TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(rr.count() + 1, pts);
                p = rr.end();
                ++rings;
            }
            TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_PTR(end, p);
        }
    TEST_ASSERT_GREATER_THAN(1000, rings);
}

static void test_known_tiles() {
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(int(TileRef::Land), int(builtinTiles().tile(tileRow(24.5), tileCol(45.0)).kind));    // Najd
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(int(TileRef::Water), int(builtinTiles().tile(tileRow(30.5), tileCol(-140.5)).kind)); // Pacific
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(int(TileRef::Rings), int(builtinTiles().tile(tileRow(37.6), tileCol(-122.4)).kind)); // SF Bay
    // Antarctica is left out: no tile south of 60° S has data.
    for (int r = 0; r < kFirstDataRow; ++r)
        for (int c = 0; c < kCols; ++c) TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(int(TileRef::Water), int(builtinTiles().tile(r, c).kind));
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(gen::kRowRank[kFirstDataRow] == 0);
}

static void test_airports_sorted_with_runways() {
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(10, int(sizeof(gen::Airport)));
    TEST_ASSERT_GREATER_THAN(2000, gen::kAirportCount);
    int runways = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < gen::kAirportCount; ++i) {
        const gen::Airport& a = gen::kAirports[i];
        if (i) TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(gen::kAirports[i - 1].latE5() <= a.latE5());
        TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(a.latE5() >= -9000000 && a.latE5() <= 9000000);
        TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(a.lonE5() >= -18000000 && a.lonE5() <= 18000000);
        char id[5];
        a.ident(id);
        TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(std::strlen(id) >= 3);
        if (!a.large()) TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(0, a.runways());
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(runways, a.firstRunway());
        runways += a.runways();
    }
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(gen::kRunwayCount, runways);
}

static const gen::Airport* find(const char* code) {
    char id[5];
    for (int i = 0; i < gen::kAirportCount; ++i)
        if (gen::kAirports[i].ident(id), !std::strcmp(id, code)) return &gen::kAirports[i];
    return nullptr;
}

// The packed records decode to the real positions; runway ends land near the airport.
static void test_known_airports() {
    const gen::Airport* sfo = find("KSFO");
    TEST_ASSERT_NOT_NULL(sfo);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(sfo->large());
    TEST_ASSERT_INT32_WITHIN(3, 3761981, sfo->latE5());  // 37.619806, -122.374821 in airports.csv
    TEST_ASSERT_INT32_WITHIN(3, -12237482, sfo->lonE5());
    TEST_ASSERT_GREATER_OR_EQUAL(4, sfo->runways());
    for (int k = 0; k < sfo->runways(); ++k) {
        const gen::Runway& r = gen::kRunways[sfo->firstRunway() + k];
        TEST_ASSERT_INT32_WITHIN(5000, 0, r.dLat1);  // within ~5 km of the reference point
        TEST_ASSERT_INT32_WITHIN(5000, 0, r.dLon2);
    }
    const gen::Airport* bos = find("KBOS");
    TEST_ASSERT_NOT_NULL(bos);
    TEST_ASSERT_INT32_WITHIN(3, 4236197, bos->latE5());  // 42.36197, -71.0079 in airports.csv
    TEST_ASSERT_INT32_WITHIN(3, -7100790, bos->lonE5());
    TEST_ASSERT_NOT_NULL(find("EGLL"));
}

static void test_flash_budget() {
    const int ringTiles = gen::kRowRank[kRows];
    const int runwayStarts = (gen::kAirportCount + gen::kRunwayBlock - 1) / gen::kRunwayBlock;
    size_t bytes = gen::kRingDataSize + (ringTiles + 1) * sizeof(uint32_t) + 2 * gen::kBitBytes + sizeof gen::kRowRank +
                   gen::kAirportCount * sizeof(gen::Airport) + runwayStarts * sizeof(uint16_t) +
                   gen::kRunwayCount * sizeof(gen::Runway);
    TEST_ASSERT_LESS_OR_EQUAL(360u * 1024u, bytes);
}

int main() {
    UNITY_BEGIN();
    RUN_TEST(test_index_is_consistent);
    RUN_TEST(test_every_tile_decodes);
    RUN_TEST(test_known_tiles);
    RUN_TEST(test_airports_sorted_with_runways);
    RUN_TEST(test_known_airports);
    RUN_TEST(test_flash_budget);
    return UNITY_END();
}
```

#include <unity.h>

#include "map/LandMask.h"

using namespace basemap;

void setUp() {}
void tearDown() {}

static uint8_t bits[LandMask::kBytes];

// Tile (45, 90) = lat 0..2, lon 0..2: an island (0.5..1.5°) holding a lake (~0.88..1.12°);
// its eastern neighbour (45, 91) is solid land.
static const uint8_t kIsland[] = {
    2, 0,                                                  // two rings
    4, 0, 0x00, 0x02, 0x00, 0x02,                          // island: 4 points from (512, 512)
    0x82, 0x00, 0x04, 0x00,                                // medium: +1024, 0
    0x82, 0x00, 0x00, 0x40,                                // medium: 0, +1024
    0x82, 0x00, 0x0C, 0x00,                                // medium: -1024, 0
    4, 0, 0x84, 0x03, 0x84, 0x03,                          // lake: 4 points from (900, 900)
    0x82, 0xFA, 0x00, 0x00,                                // medium: +250, 0
    0x82, 0x00, 0xA0, 0x0F,                                // medium: 0, +250
    0x82, 0x06, 0x0F, 0x00,                                // medium: -250, 0
};

class TestTiles : public TileSource {
   public:
    TileRef tile(int row, int col) const override {
        TileRef t;
        if (row == 45 && col == 90) t.kind = TileRef::Rings, t.data = kIsland, t.size = sizeof kIsland;
        if (row == 45 && col == 91) t.kind = TileRef::Land;
        return t;
    }
};

// Home at (1, 1), radius 120 nm: 1 px per nm, 60 px per degree.
static void test_island_lake_and_full_tile() {
    LandMask m(bits);
    MapView v = viewFor(1.0, 1.0, 120);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(2, m.build(v, TestTiles()));
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(m.landAtScreen(120, 135));  // home: in the lake
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(m.landAtScreen(100, 135));   // the island, west of the lake
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(m.landAtScreen(70, 135));   // sea west of the island
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(m.landAtScreen(176, 135));  // sea, just inside the home tile
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(m.landAtScreen(184, 135));   // the full tile east of it
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(m.landAtScreen(184, 16));   // north of the full tile (no data there)
}

static void test_coverage_is_smoothed_at_the_coast() {
    LandMask m(bits);
    m.build(viewFor(1.0, 1.0, 120), TestTiles());
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(0, m.coverage16(70, 135));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(16, m.coverage16(100, 135));
    int edge = m.coverage16(89, 135);  // the island's west shore is at x = 90
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(edge > 0 && edge < 16);
}

    TEST_ASSERT_FLOAT_WITHIN(0.01f, 123.f, north.y);

// The real tables: Boston Harbor is water, Cambridge across the river is land.

    LandMask m(bits);
    MapView v = viewFor(42.36, -71.06, 10);
    TEST_ASSERT_GREATER_THAN(0, m.build(v));
    gfx::Vec2 harbor = v.project(42.335, kHarborLon), cambridge = v.project(42.373, -71.119);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(m.landAtScreen(int(harbor.x), int(harbor.y)));
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(m.landAtScreen(int(cambridge.x), int(cambridge.y)));
}

static void test_open_ocean_has_no_land() {

    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(0, m.build(viewFor(30.0, -140.0, 25)));

}

    RUN_TEST(test_coverage_is_smoothed_at_the_coast);
    RUN_TEST(test_projection);
    RUN_TEST(test_boston_harbor_water_and_cambridge_land);
    RUN_TEST(test_open_ocean_has_no_land);

}

```cpp
#pragma once
// Land and water around home as a 1-bit mask at half the screen's resolution (spec §2), rebuilt when
// the map opens or home or the radius changes. Every tile the view covers is rasterised with an XOR
// scanline fill: each ring edge toggles one bit per mask row it crosses, then a running parity
// along each row turns the toggles into even-odd fill. No edge list is kept, so the mask itself is
// the only memory it needs.
#include <cstdint>

#include "Tiles.h"
#include "gfx/Canvas.h"

namespace basemap {

// The map's projection: home at (cx, cy), equirectangular with longitudes scaled by the cosine of
// home's latitude, pxPerNm screen pixels per nautical mile.
struct MapView {
    double lat = 0, lon = 0, cosLat = 1;
    float pxPerNm = 4.8f;
    float cx = 120, cy = 135;
    int w = 240, h = 270;
    gfx::Vec2 project(double plat, double plon) const;
};
// The radius spans half the map's width (decision 7).
MapView viewFor(double lat, double lon, float radiusNm);

class LandMask {
   public:
    static constexpr int kW = 120, kH = 135, kStride = kW / 8, kBytes = kStride * kH;
    LandMask() = default;
    explicit LandMask(uint8_t* bits) : bits_(bits) {}  // kBytes of storage (the screen arena)
    uint8_t* bits() const { return bits_; }
    // Rasterises the tiles the view covers; returns how many held land.
    int build(const MapView& v, const TileSource& src = builtinTiles());
    bool land(int mx, int my) const { return bits_[my * kStride + (mx >> 3)] >> (7 - (mx & 7)) & 1; }
    bool landAtScreen(int x, int y) const;
    // Land coverage at screen pixel (x, y), 0 (water) .. 16 (land): the four nearest mask pixels,
    // weighted bilinearly. A screen pixel centre sits a quarter of the way between two mask pixel
    // centres, so the weights are always 3:1 or 1:3 on each axis.
    int coverage16(int x, int y) const;

   private:
    struct Affine {
        double sx, ox, sy, oy;  // tile units to mask pixels
    };
    void edge(const Affine& a, int32_t x0, int32_t y0, int32_t x1, int32_t y1);
    void fillParity();
    uint8_t* bits_ = nullptr;
};

}  // namespace basemap
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the mask.** Create `lib/core/map/LandMask.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "LandMask.h"

#ifdef CONTRAIL_ESP32
#pragma GCC optimize("O2")  // the toggles and the parity pass are tight loops
#endif

#include <cmath>
#include <cstring>
#include <utility>

namespace basemap {

namespace {
constexpr double kPiD = 3.14159265358979323846;
}

MapView viewFor(double lat, double lon, float radiusNm) {
    MapView v;
    v.lat = lat, v.lon = lon, v.cosLat = std::cos(lat * kPiD / 180);
    v.pxPerNm = 120.f / (radiusNm > 1 ? radiusNm : 1);
    return v;
}

gfx::Vec2 MapView::project(double plat, double plon) const {
    double dlon = plon - lon;
    if (dlon > 180) dlon -= 360;
    else if (dlon < -180) dlon += 360;
    const double k = 60.0 * pxPerNm;  // a degree of latitude is 60 nm
    return {float(cx + dlon * k * cosLat), float(cy - (plat - lat) * k)};
}

int LandMask::build(const MapView& v, const TileSource& src) {
    std::memset(bits_, 0, kBytes);
    const double k = 60.0 * v.pxPerNm, kx = k * v.cosLat;  // screen pixels per degree
    // The view's extent, a mask pixel wider all round.
    const double latTop = v.lat + (v.cy + 2) / k, latBot = v.lat - (v.h - v.cy + 2) / k;
    const double lonW = v.lon - (v.cx + 2) / kx, lonE = v.lon + (v.w - v.cx + 2) / kx;
    const int r0 = tileRow(latBot), r1 = tileRow(latTop);
    const int c0 = int(std::floor((lonW + 180) / kTileDeg)), c1 = int(std::floor((lonE + 180) / kTileDeg));
    int landTiles = 0;
    for (int row = r0; row <= r1; ++row)
        for (int cu = c0; cu <= c1; ++cu) {  // unwrapped column: the projection stays continuous across 180°
            TileRef t = src.tile(row, ((cu % kCols) + kCols) % kCols);
            if (t.kind == TileRef::Water) continue;
            ++landTiles;
            const double lat0 = -90.0 + row * kTileDeg, lon0 = -180.0 + cu * kTileDeg;
            Affine a;
            a.sx = kx * kTileDeg / kUnits / 2, a.ox = (v.cx + (lon0 - v.lon) * kx) / 2;
            a.sy = -k * kTileDeg / kUnits / 2, a.oy = (v.cy - (lat0 - v.lat) * k) / 2;
            if (t.kind == TileRef::Land) {
                edge(a, 0, 0, kUnits, 0), edge(a, kUnits, 0, kUnits, kUnits);
                edge(a, kUnits, kUnits, 0, kUnits), edge(a, 0, kUnits, 0, 0);
                continue;
            }
            const int n = t.data[0] | t.data[1] << 8;
            const uint8_t* p = t.data + 2;
            const uint8_t* end = t.data + t.size;
            for (int i = 0; i < n && p < end; ++i) {
                RingReader rr(p, end);
                RingPoint prev, cur;
                if (!rr.next(prev)) break;
                while (rr.next(cur)) edge(a, prev.x, prev.y, cur.x, cur.y), prev = cur;
                if (!rr.ok()) break;
                p = rr.end();
            }
        }
    fillParity();
    return landTiles;
}

void LandMask::edge(const Affine& a, int32_t tx0, int32_t ty0, int32_t tx1, int32_t ty1) {
    float x0 = float(a.ox + a.sx * tx0), y0 = float(a.oy + a.sy * ty0);
    float x1 = float(a.ox + a.sx * tx1), y1 = float(a.oy + a.sy * ty1);
    if (y0 == y1) return;
    if (y0 > y1) std::swap(x0, x1), std::swap(y0, y1);
    // Rows whose centre (r + 0.5) lies in [y0, y1): half-open, so a vertex shared by two edges
    // toggles once.
    int r0 = int(std::ceil(y0 - 0.5f)), r1 = int(std::ceil(y1 - 0.5f)) - 1;
    if (r0 < 0) r0 = 0;
    if (r1 > kH - 1) r1 = kH - 1;
    const float slope = (x1 - x0) / (y1 - y0);
    for (int r = r0; r <= r1; ++r) {
        float xc = x0 + (r + 0.5f - y0) * slope;
        int c = int(std::floor(xc - 0.5f)) + 1;  // the first pixel whose centre is right of the crossing
        if (c >= kW) continue;
        if (c < 0) c = 0;
        bits_[r * kStride + (c >> 3)] ^= uint8_t(0x80 >> (c & 7));
    }
}

void LandMask::fillParity() {
    for (int r = 0; r < kH; ++r) {
        uint8_t parity = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < kStride; ++i) {
            // Prefix XOR within the byte from its first pixel (the most significant bit) onward.
            uint8_t v = bits_[r * kStride + i];
            v ^= v >> 1, v ^= v >> 2, v ^= v >> 4;
            if (parity) v = uint8_t(~v);
            parity = v & 1;
            bits_[r * kStride + i] = v;
        }
    }
}

bool LandMask::landAtScreen(int x, int y) const {
    int mx = x >> 1, my = y >> 1;
    return mx >= 0 && mx < kW && my >= 0 && my < kH && land(mx, my);
}

int LandMask::coverage16(int x, int y) const {
    const int i0 = (x - 1) >> 1, j0 = (y - 1) >> 1;  // arithmetic shift: x = 0 gives -1, clamped below
    const int wx = (x & 1) ? 3 : 1, wy = (y & 1) ? 3 : 1;
    auto at = [&](int i, int j) {
        i = i < 0 ? 0 : i >= kW ? kW - 1 : i;
        j = j < 0 ? 0 : j >= kH ? kH - 1 : j;
        return int(land(i, j));
    };
    return wy * (wx * at(i0, j0) + (4 - wx) * at(i0 + 1, j0)) +
           (4 - wy) * (wx * at(i0, j0 + 1) + (4 - wx) * at(i0 + 1, j0 + 1));
}

}  // namespace basemap
```
